## Evidence Standards Used

- Highest weight: dated public IDA MCP function bounds, bytes, decompilation, disassembly, public `callees`, `xref_query.total`, stack frames, item boundaries, strings, RTTI/COL structures, and independent name catalogs; any authorized transaction repeats the required reads freshly.
- Corroboration: constructor allocation size, vptr stores, singleton reads/writes, dated generated CPP/H snapshots, callback-applied by-* ownership metadata, exact neighboring ranges, manual coverage rows, and archived accepted reports. Mutable generated and ordinary artifacts require a fresh physical reread whenever a later gate relies on them.
- Negative evidence: full-name catalogs, exact VA/RVA byte-pattern searches, zero-xref helper checks, the historical pre-callback missing generated header, absence of alternate file owner, and neighboring function/data exclusion.
- Public `callees` is direct-callee authority because aggregate profiles can include self edges. `xref_query.total` is incoming-xref authority because modeled caller counts can omit unmodeled sites.
- Confidence is not reduced merely because private names are inferred; behavior, layout, boundaries, source placement, and formal output are independently closed.

## Positive Evidence Summary

- Nine exact local functions, six internal alignment spans, and two switch tables fill `[0x494520,0x4949df)` with no unexplained byte. The seven-byte predecessor and one-byte successor padding are separately owned by ignored padding.
- Constructor allocation size `0x128`, three vptr stores, two type descriptors, three COLs, two CHDs, two BCAs, eight BCD bodies, the seven-base hierarchy, and exact field accesses converge on one `Pane, Singleton<ConnStatusPane>` class layout.
- Primary OnPaint slot, secondary packet-event slot, A0/A4 destructor adjustors, and primary deleting destructor tie every class-local virtual body to the three vtable views.
- Exact strings, public callees, config/map gate, event packet layout, TimerMgr tick field, and two updater calls support complete method behavior.
- Four singleton xrefs prove constructor publication, two class-local clears, and one external MapPane teardown read, requiring external linkage.
- Fresh `type_inspect` proves the IDA prerequisites `Event`, `EventHandler`, `TimerHandler`, and `Pane` already exist; only the incomplete `ConnStatusPane` name is absent. This is IDA type evidence only, not C++ header-closure proof. The accepted callback independently completed C138's `Config.h`/`StringUtil.h` complete-type repair and C139's guarded, placeholder-free `TimerMgr.h` route.
- Dated command-header 27513 physical headers positively confirmed those routes without weakening method semantics: `Config.h` contained `m_mapMovementStatusOption`, `g_pConfig`, and the complete vector route; `StringUtil.h` retained the UID0000OB owner and ordered sibling class fragments; `TimerMgr.h` retained exact public `DWORD m_currentTick` and `g_pTimerMgr`. Review-time command-27631 and final repair-time command-27648 ConnStatusPane CPP/H rereads confirm the completed consuming unit. The callback changed declaration placement, guard/child composition, and include closure only, not fields, layout, ownership, or ConnStatusPane behavior.
- The clean failed Gate 2B A00 readback strengthens that prerequisite proof: physical `Event` is size 272 and its exact protected rows are `vftable`, `m_type`, `_gap5`, and `m_payload`; `EventHandler`, `TimerHandler`, and `Pane` retained their accepted shapes, and `ConnStatusPane` alone was absent. Because the transaction stopped before declaration, this is read-only evidence rather than mutation credit.
- Fresh A02/A04/A09 reads close the earlier literal-prestate issues without making any post-decompile type universal. A02 retains its exact clean-first-to-Pane stage contract. A04, A07, A08, and A09 are each closed by a finite R0/RP contract: R0 retains the prior raw/presentation pair through both ordered cycles, while RP changes only the row-specific item/analyze type in cycle 1 and requires identical cycle 2. Every branch retains the same frame, comments, bytes/seal, bounds, xrefs, caller/callee sets, tail/body, and semantics; selected branches survive dry and persistent rename, then converge only at C146/C152/C154/C156 before branch-free comment action. Transactions 092311/163400/1901/T0920Z plus B003's independent earlier rechecks supply zero mutation or persistence credit.
- A03's pre-action contradiction is closed by stabilization rather than an asserted untouched item type. Fresh canonical-derived sessions returned both signed and unsigned initial inspect values, while direct decompile consistently rendered unsigned; in session 211736 it changed the next inspect to unsigned, after which two complete decompile-first cycles were identical. T1722Z separately proves a bounded post-C104 split: desired name in item/disassembly/analysis registries with exact stale `sub_494610` declaration text in direct decompile and `analyze_function.decompiled` across three readbacks. The selected `void` post-type remains supported by exact store-only behavior and absence of any modeled return consumer; historical 1110 convergence and C144's literal named signature support making exact `set_type` the sole convergence action, with named readback still mandatory.
- D01's one-call contradiction is closed by an exact serial public-endpoint contract. Fresh 1126 reads prove the untouched physical/xref/comment prestate; the unsaved 1110 result proves `make_data` deterministically supplies the intended four-byte boundary/name while retaining `Pane *`; current schema and tested equivalent singleton evidence support immediate global `set_type` from that exact intermediate. The final source-facing `ConnStatusPane *` type, four xrefs, zero bytes, and all neighbors remain unchanged; only primitive action/readback topology changes.
- Cross-session D02 evidence strengthens rather than changes the resource conclusion: M1/M2 metadata profiles and both classifier branches preserve the same two physical items, unchanged 26-byte UTF-16 vector, sole incoming OnPaint xref, and `NNSTAT.EPD` suffix. Branch B's sole outgoing target is scalar value `0x004f0043` encoded by the first four literal bytes and lands inside a two-byte instruction, proving it is a false item-model artifact. One source literal and one merged final IDA object remain deterministic even though no read-order barrier forces one metadata profile or classifier branch.

## Documentation Evidence And IDA Status

- The pre-callback supporting-document snapshot agreed on class purpose, network path, method island, singleton, Pane base, and broad latency behavior.
- Historical stale or incomplete text included provisional `ui/status` language, old command 7318, unsigned/C++11 draft source, explicit singleton writes in methods, invented packet/render helpers, `currentTick` spelling, missing header/formal class source, malformed vtable boundary, and the old claim that UID00035G includes a locator at `0x617a34`; the accepted ordinary callback repaired those documented defects.
- The dated read-only IDA evidence materially improved that historical documentation: the complete three-view RTTI hierarchy, exact signed layout, exact vtable cells, exact switch tables/padding, direct-callee authority, helper negative evidence, physical resource split, and singleton item prestate were not all recorded together before.
- The later clean A00 failure corrected only the physical prerequisite spelling contract: `_gap5` and `m_payload` are authoritative current `Event` member names; the former `gap5` and `payload` requirements are rejected dated report history. No IDA action occurred, and no ordinary document contradicts the correction because current source already uses `m_payload` with compiler-provided alignment.
- The clean A02/A04/A09 failures corrected public clean-first prestates without changing source semantics or ordinary documentation. A02 retains one required refinement. #ifndef NEXUSTK_NETWORK_CONNSTATUSPANE_H
#define NEXUSTK_NETWORK_CONNSTATUSPANE_H

#include "../ui/core/Pane.h"
#include "../util/Singleton.h"

class Event;

class ConnStatusPane : public Pane, public Singleton<ConnStatusPane>
{
public:
    ConnStatusPane();
    virtual ~ConnStatusPane();

protected:
    virtual void OnPaint();
    virtual bool HandlePacketEvent(Event *event);

private:

    void UpdateConnectionFrame();

    int m_pendingLatencyStartTick;
    short m_connectionFrame;
};

Exact UID000113 `RECONSTRUCTION_CPP CODE` replacement; its H block remains blank because declarations belong in UID000037 H:

```cpp
namespace
{
const int kInvalidLatencySample = -1;
const short kInvalidConnectionFrame = -1;
const short kMapStatusConnectionFrame = 4;
const int kLatencyFastThresholdMs = 400;
const int kLatencyMidThresholdMs = 800;
const int kLatencySlowThresholdMs = 1200;
}

ConnStatusPane::ConnStatusPane()
    : Pane(1)
{
    for (int index = 0; index < 10; ++index)
        m_latencySamples[index] = kInvalidLatencySample;

    m_pendingLatencyStartTick = kInvalidLatencySample;
    m_connectionFrame = kInvalidConnectionFrame;
}

ConnStatusPane::~ConnStatusPane()
{
}

void ConnStatusPane::CaptureLatencyStartTick()
{
    m_pendingLatencyStartTick = static_cast<int>(g_pTimerMgr->m_currentTick);
}

void ConnStatusPane::OnPaint()
{
    if (m_connectionFrame == kInvalidConnectionFrame)
    {
        SetDrawMode(0);
        SetDrawColor(0);
        g_pfnFillRect(this, &m_visibleBounds);
        return;
    }

    EPFTileContext tile;
    g_pEPFLib->LookupLayoutEntry(
        L"CONNSTAT.EPD", m_connectionFrame, &tile);
    RenderTileFrame(
        &tile, &tile.bounds, &m_visibleBounds, 0, L"NPAL7.PAL", 0);
}

bool ConnStatusPane::HandlePacketEvent(Event *event)
{
    const unsigned char packetType =
        *static_cast<const unsigned char *>(event->m_payload.m_packet.m_data);

    switch (packetType)
    {
    case 4:
    case 11:
    case 21:
    case 38:
        if (g_pConfig->m_mapMovementStatusOption &&
            g_activeMapPane != 0 &&
            g_activeMapPane->AllowsMovementStatusTag())
        {
            if (m_pendingLatencyStartTick != kInvalidLatencySample)
                UpdateConnectionFrame();

            if (m_connectionFrame != kMapStatusConnectionFrame)
            {
                m_connectionFrame = kMapStatusConnectionFrame;
                InvalidateRect(&m_visibleBounds);
            }
        }
        else if (m_pendingLatencyStartTick != kInvalidLatencySample)
        {
            UpdateConnectionFrame();
        }
        break;
    }

    return false;
}

void ConnStatusPane::UpdateConnectionFrame()
{
    for (int index = 0; index < 9; ++index)
        m_latencySamples[index] = m_latencySamples[index + 1];

    m_latencySamples[9] =
        static_cast<int>(g_pTimerMgr->m_currentTick) -
        m_pendingLatencyStartTick;
    m_pendingLatencyStartTick = kInvalidLatencySample;

    int total = 0;
    int sampleCount = 0;
    for (int index = 0; index < 10; ++index)
    {
        if (m_latencySamples[index] != kInvalidLatencySample)
        {
            total += m_latencySamples[index];
            ++sampleCount;
        }
    }

    short nextFrame = kInvalidConnectionFrame;
    if (sampleCount != 0)
    {
        const int average = total / sampleCount;
        if (average < kLatencyFastThresholdMs)
            nextFrame = 3;
        else if (average < kLatencyMidThresholdMs)
            nextFrame = 2;
        else if (average < kLatencySlowThresholdMs)
            nextFrame = 1;
        else
            nextFrame = 0;
    }

    if (m_connectionFrame != nextFrame)
    {
        m_connectionFrame = nextFrame;
        InvalidateRect(&m_visibleBounds);
    }
}
```
